# Unit 13 linguistic and owned-edition mapping packet

This packet prepares review. It records an unsigned structural mapping proposal. It does not record a qualified review, an approved edition alignment, or an install decision.

The owned-edition mapping worksheet separates observed edition facts, the proposed lesson and printed page range, unresolved edition questions, and matters that still require qualified linguistic review. ISBN, owned-copy verification, variance, reviewer identity, qualification, date, and approval stay blank. The disposition remains PENDING_CURRICULUM_REVIEWER.

Unit 13 remains in development. Its readiness is content-complete and review-pending. The Lesson 13 relationship is a proposed, unsigned mapping. It is not an approved edition alignment.

`packContentReviewDigest` is the repository aggregate of instructional curriculum. It is not an approval of this unit, and it is not part of the review-package digest.

`repositorySourceDigest` binds repository and release inputs. Recording an approval later changes that repository hash. That change does not replace the signed content-review digest or the mapping-package digest.

kind=TEACH
title=Admitted instruction
body=In English, when reporting a story, we usually change the tense of what was said: compare "I ''am'' English" with "He said he ''was'' English" - the reported verb changes tense. In Russian, however, we keep the tense the same, so the same report is literally, "He said he '''is''' English" - "'''Он сказа́л, что он англича́нин'''", not "'''Он сказа́л, что он ''был'' англича́нином'''".
dominantAction=Continue
references=ref-u1115-attribution
items=
examples=
unavailableReason=

kind=TEACH
title=Interpret the nationality report
body=A past reporting frame can contain a statement whose tense is interpreted from the reported speaker’s perspective. Он сказа́л, что он англича́нин has a past said verb and no past copula in the nationality statement. The supplied original is “I am English”. Past reporting verb with present nationality statement. is the matching description. The source’s alternative with a past copula changes the stated reading. That alternative can be grammatical in a different context; it is not this supplied present-nationality report. This nationality context was also taught earlier, so it is not unseen evidence.

kind=TEACH
title=Separate reporting time from embedded time
body=Я скажу ему ве́чером is the direct statement “I will tell him in the evening”. Она́ сказа́ла, что она́ ска́жет ему́ ве́чером is the supplied reported evening context. Past reporting verb with a future embedded action. describes its two finite verbs: said is past, and the embedded telling action retains future form within reported content. This does not imply the action must still be future compared with the present moment. The source supplies these speaker/pronoun choices; do not mechanically replace every I with she or manufacture new reported clauses.
dominantAction=Continue
references=ref-u1115-attribution
items=
examples=
unavailableReason=
bookDirection=
companionDirection=

kind=TEACH
title=Distinguish a report, direct statement and time clause
body=Когда́ я бу́ду в Москве́, я бу́ду говори́ть то́лько по-ру́сски gives a future time clause and a plan to speak Russian. It has no past reporting frame. Existing independent choices distinguish this supplied Moscow plan and the direct evening statement. The added independent check must also interpret the evening report’s past reporting verb and future embedded action. English backshift is not compulsory in every report; preserving these source readings is not a universal rule that every tense or word stays unchanged. This small set supports recognition of supplied contexts, not unrestricted reported-speech production.

body=A past reporting frame can contain a statement whose tense is interpreted from the reported speaker’s perspective. Он сказа́л, что он англича́нин has a past said verb and no past copula in the nationality statement. The supplied original is “I am English”. Past reporting verb with present nationality statement. is the matching description. The source’s alternative with a past copula changes the stated reading. That alternative can be grammatical in a different context; it is not this supplied present-nationality report. This nationality context was also taught earlier, so it is not unseen evidence.

Я скажу ему ве́чером is the direct statement “I will tell him in the evening”. Она́ сказа́ла, что она́ ска́жет ему́ ве́чером is the supplied reported evening context. Past reporting verb with a future embedded action. describes its two finite verbs: said is past, and the embedded telling action retains future form within reported content. This does not imply the action must still be future compared with the present moment. The source supplies these speaker/pronoun choices; do not mechanically replace every I with she or manufacture new reported clauses.

Когда́ я бу́ду в Москве́, я бу́ду говори́ть то́лько по-ру́сски gives a future time clause and a plan to speak Russian. It has no past reporting frame. Existing independent choices distinguish this supplied Moscow plan and the direct evening statement. The added independent check must also interpret the evening report’s past reporting verb and future embedded action. English backshift is not compulsory in every report; preserving these source readings is not a universal rule that every tense or word stays unchanged. This small set supports recognition of supplied contexts, not unrestricted reported-speech production.

feedback=In Она́ сказа́ла, что она́ ска́жет ему́ ве́чером, Past reporting verb with a future embedded action. The reporting verb said is past; the embedded verb about telling him retains future form. Recognize this supplied context; English backshift and Russian tense/pronoun choices are not automatic rules for every report.

originalSourceText=Retained Wikibooks CC BY-SA 4.0: Russian/Grammar/Past tense oldid4567337, catalog/sources/units-11-15/wikibooks/past-tense-4567337.txt, complete Usage/imperfective/perfective and Regular conjugation contexts; Russian/Verbal Aspect oldid4059032, catalog/sources/units-11-15/wikibooks/verbal-aspect-4059032.txt, complete Introduction, Forming aspectual pairs and Unpredictable pairs contexts, including the explicit observation that a completed action can be viewed as process; Russian/Grammar/Future tense oldid3353822, catalog/sources/units-11-15/wikibooks/future-tense-3353822.txt, complete Present or future reported-nationality/evening/Moscow context. Retained OpenRussian commit50e210c4803237779cb562bc1abcea529066031c, catalog/sources/units-3-5/openrussian/verbs.csv, CC BY-SA 4.0: читать/прочитать/говорить/сказать/делать/сделать aspect and partner fields, читать past_m/past_f, прочитать past_m, сказать past_f/presfut_sg3. Source errors/overgeneralizations are not adopted: being incorrectly classified as perfective; contradictory claims about how many verbs have pairs; automatic prefix-based pairing; simple English past/progressive mapped one-to-one to Russian aspect; every reported tense retained regardless of context. The говорить/сказать pairing is bounded to the source say/tell grouping and populated lexical partner fields, not every meaning of speak or talk. Reported examples distinguish past reporting from future content, present-time nationality and future time clauses; labels classify the supplied examples rather than prescribe mechanical pronoun/tense rewriting. All scored/source tuples and exact Russian spellings/capitalization remain unchanged; original category cues/duplicate relationships and productive-competence concerns remain open. No new Russian forms or phrases. Independently authored English on 2026-10-13 within content-audit-2026-10-08; no copied primary prose or private Penguin text; final semantic audit pending.
сказа́ть is the perfective partner paired with говори́ть for the say/tell meaning shown here. де́лать is imperfective and belongs to the different do/make pair. The pairing here does not mean every use of speak or talk can be replaced by the same perfective verb.

я прочитал is the perfective past option: it presents reading as a completed whole. я читал is imperfective past, presenting the reading activity or process without asserting that completed whole. Both are past forms. English simple past wording alone does not determine Russian aspect; the question explicitly asks for the perfective form.

Она́ сказа́ла, что она́ ска́жет ему́ ве́чером is the reported evening example: a past she said frame contains a future statement about telling him in the evening. The Moscow option is a future time clause and a plan to speak Russian, with no past reporting frame. The source supplies this pair; it is not a rule to replace every first-person pronoun mechanically with she.

The same tense label fits this nationality report: we keep the tense the same. Он сказа́л, что он англича́нин has a past reporting verb but a present-time nationality statement; a past reporting verb does not itself require English-style backshift in Russian. The other label describes changing the reported tense in the English comparison. This is the shown example, not a rule that every report must preserve every tense or word unchanged.

я читал is the imperfective past option, presenting reading as activity or process. я прочитал presents the reading as a completed whole and is perfective past. A completed activity can still be described with imperfective aspect when the focus is on the process; English simple past can also translate an imperfective verb.

Я скажу ему ве́чером is the direct evening statement, meaning I will tell him in the evening. It has a first-person future verb without a past reporting frame. The shown reported example instead says that she said she would tell him. The Moscow option describes a different future plan, not this direct evening statement.

originalSourceText=Retained Wikibooks CC BY-SA 4.0: Russian/Grammar/Past tense oldid4567337, catalog/sources/units-11-15/wikibooks/past-tense-4567337.txt, complete Usage/imperfective/perfective and Regular conjugation contexts; Russian/Verbal Aspect oldid4059032, catalog/sources/units-11-15/wikibooks/verbal-aspect-4059032.txt, complete Introduction, Forming aspectual pairs and Unpredictable pairs contexts, including the explicit observation that a completed action can be viewed as process; Russian/Grammar/Future tense oldid3353822, catalog/sources/units-11-15/wikibooks/future-tense-3353822.txt, complete Present or future reported-nationality/evening/Moscow context. Retained OpenRussian commit50e210c4803237779cb562bc1abcea529066031c, catalog/sources/units-3-5/openrussian/verbs.csv, CC BY-SA 4.0: читать/прочитать/говорить/сказать/делать/сделать aspect and partner fields, читать past_m/past_f, прочитать past_m, сказать past_f/presfut_sg3. Source errors/overgeneralizations are not adopted: being incorrectly classified as perfective; contradictory claims about how many verbs have pairs; automatic prefix-based pairing; simple English past/progressive mapped one-to-one to Russian aspect; every reported tense retained regardless of context. The говорить/сказать pairing is bounded to the source say/tell grouping and populated lexical partner fields, not every meaning of speak or talk. Reported examples distinguish past reporting from future content, present-time nationality and future time clauses; labels classify the supplied examples rather than prescribe mechanical pronoun/tense rewriting. All scored/source tuples and exact Russian spellings/capitalization remain unchanged; original category cues/duplicate relationships and productive-competence concerns remain open. No new Russian forms or phrases. Independently authored English on 2026-10-13 within content-audit-2026-10-08; no copied primary prose or private Penguin text; final semantic audit pending.
Когда́ я бу́ду в Москве́, я бу́ду говори́ть то́лько по-ру́сски is the future plan about Moscow: when the speaker is there, the speaker will speak only Russian. Both clauses refer to the future, including the clause meaning when. Я скажу ему ве́чером instead says I will tell him in the evening. Neither option is itself a past reporting frame; identify the Moscow plan requested here.

Neither option is itself a past reporting frame; identify the Moscow plan requested here.

recordType=label
sourceUrl=urn:russian-companion:content-audit-research:2026-10-08
sourceRevision=content-audit-2026-10-08

originalSourceText=Complete retained Wikibooks Future3353822 Present or future subsection with nationality comparison and direct/reported evening examples, existing CC BY-SA4.0 contributor-history/rights route Copyrights4622060. Native clauses preserve source stress/words/punctuation; only mechanical emphasis/link-label unfolding. Independent English,2026-10-13. Bounded supplied-context recognition, not generating reports or universally mandatory English backshift/Russian tense retention/pronoun replacement. The source mislabels быть perfective and overgeneralizes aspect/completion/repetition and every original tense; those claims are excluded. Guided nationality example has already been taught earlier; new report uses existing evening context, not unseen evidence. Source/rights/linguistic acceptance pending; no private book prose.
A past reporting frame can contain a statement whose tense is interpreted from the reported speaker’s perspective. Он сказа́л, что он англича́нин has a past said verb and no past copula in the nationality statement. The supplied original is “I am English”. Past reporting verb with present nationality statement. is the matching description. The source’s alternative with a past copula changes the stated reading. That alternative can be grammatical in a different context; it is not this supplied present-nationality report. This nationality context was also taught earlier, so it is not unseen evidence.

Я скажу ему ве́чером is the direct statement “I will tell him in the evening”. Она́ сказа́ла, что она́ ска́жет ему́ ве́чером is the supplied reported evening context. Past reporting verb with a future embedded action. describes its two finite verbs: said is past, and the embedded telling action retains future form within reported content. This does not imply the action must still be future compared with the present moment. The source supplies these speaker/pronoun choices; do not mechanically replace every I with she or manufacture new reported clauses.

Когда́ я бу́ду в Москве́, я бу́ду говори́ть то́лько по-ру́сски gives a future time clause and a plan to speak Russian. It has no past reporting frame. Existing independent choices distinguish this supplied Moscow plan and the direct evening statement. Interpret the complete supplied context. A missed choice returns to all teaching cards and worked examples.

sourceRecordId=future::I will tell him in the evening - '''Я скажу ему ве́чером'''
:She said that she 
originalSourceText=:I will tell him in the evening - '''Я скажу ему ве́чером'''
:She said that she would speak to him in the evening - '''Она́ сказа́ла, что она́ ска́жет ему́ ве́чером''' - ''perfective past and future as actions completed, definite, etc.''
:When I'm in Moscow, I'll speak only in Russian - '''Когда́ я бу́ду в Москве́, я бу́ду говори́ть то́лько по-ру́сски'''

recordType=meaning
sourceUrl=https://en.wikibooks.org/wiki/Russian/Grammar/Future_tense?oldid=3353822
sourceRevision=3353822
sourceRecordId=future:In English, when reporting a story, we usually change the tense of what was said
originalSourceText=In English, when reporting a story, we usually change the tense of what was said: compare "I ''am'' English" with "He said he ''was'' English" - the reported verb changes tense. In Russian, however, we keep the tense the same, so the same report is literally, "He said he '''is''' English" - "'''Он сказа́л, что он англича́нин'''", not "'''Он сказа́л, что он ''был'' англича́нином'''".
shippedText=we keep the tense the same
transformation=selection
licenseId=CC BY-SA 4.0
licenseUrl=https://creativecommons.org/licenses/by-sa/4.0/

originalSourceText=In English, when reporting a story, we usually change the tense of what was said: compare "I ''am'' English" with "He said he ''was'' English" - the reported verb changes tense. In Russian, however, we keep the tense the same, so the same report is literally, "He said he '''is''' English" - "'''Он сказа́л, что он англича́нин'''", not "'''Он сказа́л, что он ''был'' англича́нином'''".

:She said that she 
originalSourceText=:I will tell him in the evening - '''Я скажу ему ве́чером'''
:She said that she would speak to him in the evening - '''Она́ сказа́ла, что она́ ска́жет ему́ ве́чером''' - ''perfective past and future as actions completed, definite, etc.''
:When I'm in Moscow, I'll speak only in Russian - '''Когда́ я бу́ду в Москве́, я бу́ду говори́ть то́лько по-ру́сски'''

sourceRecordId=past:So the English ''past progressive'' sentence  "I was reading" would correspond t
originalSourceText=So the English ''past progressive'' sentence  "I was reading" would correspond to the Russian imperfective ('''я читал'''), since it refers to an action that wasn't completed. Likewise, the English ''simple past'' sentence "I read" corresponds to the Russian perfective ('''я прочитал''')

recordType=example
sourceUrl=https://en.wikibooks.org/wiki/Russian/Grammar/Future_tense?oldid=3353822
sourceRevision=3353822
sourceRecordId=future-report-full-context:83ebe0a793af0039623aecdf1a0b3e548ba94af3d8ef89259089cc01791807b1
originalSourceText==== Present or future? ===

There is also the decision whether to use the future tense at all, and there are three nuances between English and Russian. In English, when reporting a story, we usually change the tense of what was said: compare "I ''am'' English" with "He said he ''was'' English" - the reported verb changes tense. In Russian, however, we keep the tense the same, so the same report is literally, "He said he '''is''' English" - "'''Он сказа́л, что он англича́нин'''", not "'''Он сказа́л, что он ''был'' англича́нином'''". Whatever tense was used originally is kept.

Another nuance between English and Russian is using prepositions and time. In English, after 'if', 'until', 'while', 'when', etc, we use the present tense, even if we're referring to the future. For example, in "If I fly, I'll phone you", "I fly" is the present tense, even though we're referring to the future. Since Russian has less ambiguous grammar for tense and aspect, if they're referring to the future with those words, they use it: "If I ''will fly'', I'll phone you" - '''Если я ''полечу́'', Я тебе́ позвоню́'''.

Finally, like in English, Russians can also use the present tense to refer to future events, if they're definitely going to happen: I'm going to America tomorrow - '''За́втра я ''е́ду'' в Аме́рику''', is present tense.

Examples:
:I will tell him in the evening - '''Я скажу ему ве́чером'''
:She said that she would speak to him in the evening - '''Она́ сказа́ла, что она́ ска́жет ему́ ве́чером''' - ''perfective past and future as actions completed, definite, etc.''
:When I'm in Moscow, I'll speak only in Russian - '''Когда́ я бу́ду в Москве́, я бу́ду говори́ть то́лько по-ру́сски'''
shippedText=Она́ сказа́ла, что она́ ска́жет ему́ ве́чером
transformation=selection
licenseId=CC BY-SA 4.0
licenseUrl=https://creativecommons.org/licenses/by-sa/4.0/
attribution=Wikibooks Russian grammar contributors, CC BY-SA 4.0. Page history for each frozen oldid identifies the contributors.
shareAlikeTreatment=Adapted curriculum data, selection, and arrangement are distributed under CC BY-SA 4.0. Application code, user data, and user recordings are outside that statement. No audio is relicensed because this unit ships no audio.

In Russian, however, we keep the tense the same, so the same report is literally, "He said he '''is''' English" - "'''Он сказа́л, что он англича́нин'''", not "'''Он сказа́л, что он ''был'' англича́нином'''". Another nuance between English and Russian is using prepositions and time. In English, after 'if', 'until', 'while', 'when', etc, we use the present tense, even if we're referring to the future. For example, in "If I fly, I'll phone you", "I fly" is the present tense, even though we're referring to the future. Since Russian has less ambiguous grammar for tense and aspect, if they're referring to the future with those words, they use it: "If I ''will fly'', I'll phone you" - '''Если я ''полечу́'', Я тебе́ позвоню́'''.

recordType=instruction
sourceUrl=https://en.wikibooks.org/wiki/Russian/Grammar/Future_tense?oldid=3353822
sourceRevision=3353822
sourceRecordId=future:In English, when reporting a story, we usually change the tense of what was said
originalSourceText=In English, when reporting a story, we usually change the tense of what was said: compare "I ''am'' English" with "He said he ''was'' English" - the reported verb changes tense. In Russian, however, we keep the tense the same, so the same report is literally, "He said he '''is''' English" - "'''Он сказа́л, что он англича́нин'''", not "'''Он сказа́л, что он ''был'' англича́нином'''".

recordType=instruction
sourceUrl=https://en.wikibooks.org/wiki/Russian/Grammar/Future_tense?oldid=3353822
sourceRevision=3353822
sourceRecordId=future:In English, when reporting a story, we usually change the tense of what was said
originalSourceText=In English, when reporting a story, we usually change the tense of what was said: compare "I ''am'' English" with "He said he ''was'' English" - the reported verb changes tense. In Russian, however, we keep the tense the same, so the same report is literally, "He said he '''is''' English" - "'''Он сказа́л, что он англича́нин'''", not "'''Он сказа́л, что он ''был'' англича́нином'''".
shippedText=In English, when reporting a story, we usually change the tense of what was said: compare "I ''am'' English" with "He said he ''was'' English" - the reported verb changes tense. In Russian, however, we keep the tense the same, so the same report is literally, "He said he '''is''' English" - "'''Он сказа́л, что он англича́нин'''", not "'''Он сказа́л, что он ''был'' англича́нином'''".

recordType=example
sourceUrl=https://en.wikibooks.org/wiki/Russian/Grammar/Future_tense?oldid=3353822
sourceRevision=3353822
sourceRecordId=future:In English, when reporting a story, we usually change the tense of what was said
originalSourceText=In English, when reporting a story, we usually change the tense of what was said: compare "I ''am'' English" with "He said he ''was'' English" - the reported verb changes tense. In Russian, however, we keep the tense the same, so the same report is literally, "He said he '''is''' English" - "'''Он сказа́л, что он англича́нин'''", not "'''Он сказа́л, что он ''был'' англича́нином'''".

:She said that she

:When I'm in Moscow, I'll speak only in Russian - '''Когда́ я бу́ду в Москве́, я бу́ду говори́ть то́лько по-ру́сски'''
